ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e3824	Before I speak on the Rules of Procedure to Mr Dr. Stummvoll, I may make a few comments on your speech on the Rules of Procedure, Mr Klubobmann. Firstly, of course, I will carry this issue to the next presidential meeting. On the other hand, a few clarifications on my part: In accordance with Article 52(1), paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 1 B-VG and § 90 – You have already mentioned this – the National Council is empowered to examine the management of the Federal Government, to consult its members on all matters of execution and to request all relevant information. That is perfectly clear and undisputed. The subject-matter of enforcement, i.e. the subject-matter of the right of question, is specified by the fact that it is in particular government acts, administrative matters or administrative matters of the federal government as a body of private rights. This is also set out in the Rules of Procedure. In the case of oral questions, according to § 95 in conjunction with § 90 of the Rules of Procedure, the following conditions of admissibility must be met – all of you know that, but I repeat it –: Short and concrete questions, no classification into several questions, relevant information, subject of execution. That would, as it were, be the short summary. The decision to comply with the conditions of an oral question shall be taken by the President or the President. It is in this context – and that is particularly important to me! – without any doubt, the Rules of Procedure shall apply equally to all. This is an essential and important concern for me. In general, I note that when preparing Question Time within the limits set by the law, a consensusal procedure is sought, i.e. I am very interested in the fact that all political groups share the assessment I have to make. Of course, this consensual procedure has its limits where the legal basis will be left. In the context of the Rules of Procedure, we have already discussed several times – not least yesterday at a first reading – how necessary the reform of Question Time and Question Time would be here in plenary. According to the previous parliamentary practice, requests for a political attitude or opinion, as well as for a forward-looking forecast, without having an underlying link to a concrete administrative act, are not allowed. I know how difficult such demarcations are. I have therefore already told Parliament's Directorate to be even more careful in applying the relevant regulations in the future. We will, of course, discuss this again in the President-in-Office. It was not possible in all cases for me to allow Mrs Lichtenecker's question to be examined on the one hand. I would also have the question of Mr Dr. Not allowed to speak stupidly would not have made a textual amendment here. I know that mistakes have always happened in the past. The problem is that due to past misconceptions, I cannot make misconceptions in the future. That would be my note. We will continue to discuss in the Presidency. Mr Dr. Stummvoll, you have spoken. - Please.

This is a security barrier we have. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Second point: I would like to make a reference to the report of the product piracy itself to the silent accusation made by Mr Zanger that the Austrian economy thereby takes too little light. This report makes it very clear what rights are being violated. Of the total of 418 applications, 144 are national applications – including, of course, those of commercial firms that sell exclusively certain products, especially in the clothing sector – and 274 Community applications. Of the 274 Community applications, only seven concern Austrian applications. This is what you see in the following: As an Austrian financial administration and customs, we defend the economic interests of many EU countries by also removing those articles from the market which constitute a violation of the rights of companies established in other EU countries. We expect the same from the other EU countries, and in most cases we can announce the fulfilment of this expectation, namely that the customs authorities of the other EU Member States defend Austrian rights in the same intensity as we do at our customs. Third point: If you look at Tables 7 and 8 in the report, you can see that both the number of cases and the number of fake articles is the country of origin of India and the second or third place the country of origin of China. This is therefore an important factor because many of the drug counterfeits come from India and therefore a special attention must be paid to them. We will do everything we can to increase the monitoring of this by better means and better methods in the future. I can say at this point that I myself have tried to achieve cooperation with Hong Kong, from where the largest flow of goods in containers from China comes towards Europe, with the customs authorities there. It is important that we receive early information from IT systems, such as irregularities in container loading and similar, which then results in positive results in Europe – and, of course, also in Austria – on the catch-up statistics. In this cooperation beyond the continents, we see the best condition for the successful continuation of this struggle. With regard to Mrs Hradecsni's question, as regards information and information on the homepage: I should like to point out at this point that under “www.bmf.gv.at”, under “Custom” and “Product Piracy”, there is a relatively large amount of information. In itself, the EU Commission's calculation is based on sources, including the U.S. Secret Service, the credit companies, the U.S. drug search. Of course, the estimates are. But remember, Mrs Colleague: In the case of heroin, which is strictly persecuted nowadays, which must be hidden in the jungle and where almost military means are tried to accompany the process from the source of production and to prevent high negotiating pressure that means that the costs of production are far higher, the costs of production are much higher – as strange as this is. Sounds – as if you are putting five blue pills in a pack, which can still be done in a small cellar laboratory – as the honourable Member Maier described it, it makes sense that such discrepancies occur. One does not see the internal value of a drug, namely the research that is inside to bring a drug to market maturity and approval. Production itself is usually not the high cost factor, but research and development are. It is therefore plausible that such a high difference lies in the profit margin because the production costs very little. We're trying to increase the pressure of the warrant. If we manage to raise awareness of this even in the producer countries, especially in India and China, we will be able to achieve an important partial progress in the fight against piracy. To sum up, you can say: The Austrian Parliament, the Austrian execution, but above all the Austrian authorities involved in it, are fighting here in the interests of the health and well-being of consumers and a fight to protect the economy and thus ultimately to protect the labour force. This work is a valuable one that we will continue to intensify. We will take all the proposals for improvement into account in the past as well. Thank you.

That is to say, it is an area where we have to continue this path, with a great deal of attention, with a great deal of wisdom to find this balance. I think we have a consensus here across party borders that we should continue this balance path in the future.

This is precisely the question of double-tracks, which we have repeatedly been working on in the Committee of Inquiry, in particular in connection with the BAWAG: the double-trackness between the Austrian National Bank on the one hand and the financial market supervision on the other. While it is true that some of these weaknesses in the construction, which are indicated and criticised here in the Court's report and which have also been identified by the Committee of Inquiry, have been corrected, the cardinal problem has not been corrected. In fact, the cardinal problem lies in the design of financial market supervision in such a way that as few institutions as possible – ideally only one – are involved in audit supervision. Thankfully, we find here in the Court of Auditors' report on page 123 a list of five different models of financial market surveillance and also a detailed assessment of these five different models. I would like to take two models: the Dutch system on the one hand and the English system on the other. Both are integrated supervisory models – that is also the Austrian one – but both are models where banking and securities supervision is in one hand: according to the Dutch system in the hands of the National Bank, according to the English system in the hands of the FMA. The path that has been taken in Austria was such that, while the on-the-spot analysis has now been given to the Austrian National Bank in the context of the reform, it can also act independently in the context of its audits, but the administrative activity and the drawing of consequences still lie in the supervision of the financial market. The interface problem is not finally solved, even though the two board members we visited recently in the green club tried to make us tasteful of the new structures. There have certainly been improvements, but the basic problem still does not seem to me to be solved, so I still believe that it would be useful to give the entire bank and insurance supervision into one hand. Then the question arises: Who is better suited for this? Is it the Austrian National Bank on the one hand or is it financial market supervision on the other? I would believe that more speaks for the Oesterreichische Nationalbank, because in the case of the Oesterreichische Nationalbank, yes, it is in some place powerful, also the so-called lender of last resort, that is, that institution that can quickly shoot money into the respective bank and must, in order to prevent a crash of that bank that is affected in this case. The advantage of this merger in one hand lies in – and this also shows these different models included in this report – that the efficiency in these models, where banking and insurance supervision is in one hand, is very, very, Very high – at least higher than with all other solutions – and that the interface problem is low – is at least lower than with all other solutions. This way would also be to go in Austria. Unfortunately, the course has been set in a different direction. I do not know if this path is so reversible without further delay, but we will look very, very carefully at how the current system works with the on-the-spot check at the Austrian National Bank and the administrative activity in financial market supervision. – Thank you very much.

There, too, traffic jams do not result in fewer people driving by car or less goods being transported by truck, but rather in a greater burden on the ageing population. That's the only consequence you can look at there. That is why I am convinced that this framework plan for the road, which is very detailed and which I am happy to provide to you in all the details, is one where you may find a few projects that you do not find in your opinion in order, but which you will generally agree with, because this is about the construction of second tunnel tubes, about safety measures, about discharges by bypassing, partly even while at the same time extending public transport, in order to cause a double relief at the same time. The Court of Auditors, in its report, criticises the SVA, inter alia, as regards the duration of the proceedings. In 2005 the average duration of the first applications was 78 days. However, in more than 1,200 cases, the duration of completion was longer than 90 days, and in 124 cases for more than half a year. In 2005 a total of 1 252 caregivers or 11 percent of applicants died before the completion of the care allowance procedure. The Court therefore recommended that the duration of all non-disputed proceedings be reduced to less than three months. The rapid handling of proceedings is also an important concern of our Minister Buchinger, for whom he has successfully committed himself. There has already been a significant improvement by means of internal organizational measures. After the current evaluation of March 2008, the average processing of initial applications only takes more than 69 days and the completion of applications for increase only more than 67 days. Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately, it is essential that care benefits take a few weeks. However, the progress made is encouraging and I believe that it should also be appreciated. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e17035	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! First of all, I would like to mention that the Court of Auditors in 2000 tried to examine the AUA. Because ÖIAG had 39 percent at the time, this was refused by the AUA. We then called on the Constitutional Court to do so with regard to the state of the rule. You know that the Court of Auditors can examine companies if there are either more than 50 per cent of public participation or a government stock. This was examined by the Constitutional Court. ==References== In December 2004, four years later, the Court of Auditors was granted the right to examine the AUA. We subsequently carried out the examination. The result is now available for consultation in plenary. I would like to point out – and therefore also thank myself – that this report has been considered very positive not only by experts but also by AUA's corporate bodies. I would also like to thank you for the positive response you have received from this report. Regarding the statements made by the report and with regard to the debate, I would like to point out that regardless of what business decision is made – whether this is a strategic partner or a financial investor, or whether a stand-alone solution comes – AUA will only create a turnaround if the problems that have been raised in the past are actually solved. I would like to point out the problem of fleet harmonisation. In other words, in 2005 the AUA had 15 types of four manufacturers for a total of 118 aircraft, which, of course, caused enormous additional costs. There is a 2004 collective agreement, which has been a first step towards more efficient performance, but at the same time also includes provisions that limit flexibility and are not very beneficial for productivity. I would also like to point out that the collective agreement on and on itself provides for the flying staff to do 800 block hours. By law, the framework would have been 900 block hours; the objective of the AUA would have been 700 block hours. In fact, 645 block hours were performed in 2005. In other words, it would have been possible to save more than €22 million only if the target value of block hours was achieved by the AUA and the 180 hours below the legal level. So another point where there is a need for action. There are also other problem areas in the fuel price, i.e. with kerosene. In 2004 the AUA gave up the hedging, i.e. the collateral transactions, due to liquidity problems. When you look at how the kerosene price has developed: He was US$313 in January 2004, US$116 in March 2008. This means that there was an increase of 356,55 percent, which, of course, was also reflected in the business result. If you compare AUA here with other providers, it can be shown that insurance transactions are now carried out on the basis of the Court of Auditors’ recommendation. It is expected that up to now, approximately 20 percent of collateral transactions in this area will be subordinated. For example, if you look at Air Berlin in the area: There it is 75 percent, at which Lufthansa 83 percent. [[Call the SPÖ: Madness!]] So another problem, which will be very likely to be addressed. It's about a self-fuelling to get more free space from the OMV. And it is also to be seen here that at least 15 percent can be covered in their own needs. It is about optimizing the route network after having been too strong in the past – as far as the cost side is concerned – on the individual route. There are also methods based on the original destination base, from the passenger starting point to the passenger endpoint, where the costs are accordingly considered, i.e. the cost or the value of the overall network, to see if the net is positive, whether this route is positive or not. In other words, there are certainly a number of measures to be taken here. Otherwise, they will not create the turnaround, no matter how – whether stand-alone solutions, financial investors or strategic partners – business decisions will be made. I don't think there's anything to go to OIAG. To be carried out more closely, you have already made this urgent in the context of your debate. But one point that is also my heart is the area of examination of the social security institution of the industrial economy, where it has been shown that there are huge problems in the field of care. It has already been mentioned the duration of the proceedings. Initial applications in the field of 78 days, 71 days of increase applications; 10.9% of the caregivers have died during the procedure. If we look at a comparison here, we had longer-term procedures of 22.8 percent at the Social Insurance Institute – with “longer lasting” here over three months. At the Bundespensionsanstalt you have over 57 percent of the cases that lasted longer. In a federal country, the proportion of procedures that lasted longer than three months was 72 percent. As far as medical advice is concerned, we have shown the problem that these opinions were poorly legible, incomplete and incomplete. Thus, the quality assurance instruments have not worked sufficiently. For example, if you look at the decision-makers: There are 25 decision-makers who always focus on the basic performance, and if, for example, the basic benefit changes – someone goes into retirement – then the jurisdiction changes from country to country, and the person concerned has to be re-evaluated. Through the fragmentation of the structures, you have also here different viewing standards, disunity award criteria, disunity assessment procedures, multiple reviews, a regional uneven distribution of assessors, doctors, an increased administrative burden, which is not least at the expense of the caregivers. That is to say, it would be appropriate here to take action and to ensure that an evaluation of the achievement of the goal of care is carried out. It would be necessary to reduce decision-makers, to harmonise decision-making bases, a list of experts for all, a uniform training of experts and a uniform monitoring system. I would also like to mention in this area that first positive steps have already been taken by Federal Minister Buchinger. On the occasion of the submission of the examination, he did so on 21 January. A working group was set up on September 1, 2007, with a view to quickly correcting the lack of care benefit. The first results of this working group are now available. You know the result. I can only say that some recommendations are being implemented, some recommendations are still open, and I can ask you to see that the shortcomings that exist in the area are also dealt with. Finally, I would like to thank you for the in-depth debate and also for the recognition of the work of the Court of Auditors, which I would very much like to pass on to my employees. Thank you.

Infectious diseases remain the most common cause of death worldwide. They have been increasing again in the industrialized countries for a number of decades. A relatively large number of diseases have to be reported in Austria. Although many of them are rare, it is necessary to be constantly on the hat due to increased mobility and also due to the prevailing carelessness. The reporting obligation should not be a bureaucratic burden or an obstacle. It serves to protect the population from communicable diseases, early detection and monitoring the regional epidemiological situation. This will only allow for the rapid introduction of the necessary measures and the information of the population. A quick grasp of the sources of infection and contact points as well as appropriate measures, such as locking vaccinations, are essential. The time factor plays a crucial role in this. The management of recent measles, for example, was exemplary. The authorities have been rightly praised. An internal register according to international models should now facilitate the work and coordination of the employed authorities, i.e. at the level of bra and state government. This may also have a positive impact on reporting discipline, which could also be better internationally. According to a survey, for example, 50 percent of German doctors have never reported an infection themselves. According to this survey, only 20 percent of hospital doctors reported a self-diagnosed infection themselves. In summary, it can be said that this register is rightly seen by all parties involved as progress. It will certainly be a good service. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e18803	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! The focus is on strengthening patient rights. This Regulation lays down standards on the application and treatment of medical devices and articles, but also reporting requirements. This will then, if a medical product is qualitatively inadequate or if unexpected side effects occur or if it does not work properly, opens up the possibility for the patient to have more support in his legal invention than was possible in the past. As a result, the risks to other patients or users are significantly reduced. So far, the problem has been that the victims have been disadvantaged in such a way that there are supposed problems in the use or treatment of certain medical products. It was not easy to understand whether in fact a harmful medical product was the cause, because harmful products were not really certain for evidence because they were usually sent back to the manufacturers. This, of course, has also raised problems in clarifying the facts and has made great difficulties even in the case of claims for damages. Sometimes it was actually impossible for injured people to get damages. In the future, for reasons of suspicion, if faulty medical devices have really caused damage, the health care providers, the health care providers, the hospitals will have to deal with the legal requirements of the injured patients or their survivors. Support will be available, and it will also need to be given in order to make the operations more transparent. Furthermore, in the interests of patient safety, the continuation of the already voluntaryly existing heart-stepmaker register will be ensured. I myself was amazed that in Austria more than 10,000 new and re-implantations of heart rate makers, defibrilators, loop recorders and other implants are actually carried out in the year. In future, all of these must be personally embedded in registers and documented. These new laws close legal gaps. Responsibilities are reorganised, and the position of patients is significantly improved.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e19866	Mr President. Honourable Members of the House! Values listeners at the visitor gallery! Yesterday, the European Commission decided to abolish the ban on imports of MON810 and T25 from Austria as food or feed. Despite the very disappointing decision for me – we must say that already, although we have foreseen certain things – we have hoped in the last few weeks and months that this pan-European trend will support and help us, but despite the very disappointing decision on our import bans – and I would like to point out here – it seems to be more sensitive from the European Commission's side in the area of GMO authorisation. For the first time in the overall situation, the Commission has also expressed its support for the rejection of three other applications to EFSA in order to re-examine them and clearly take this intention into account. Of course, that's nothing we should be happy about, but that's a tiny step where we see that at least the steady drop we can't stop dripping is also sometimes hollowing the stone. I've already said: The decision to withdraw this has not come as a surprise to me, since since autumn 2007 – and we have discussed this again and again in many discussions – the one-year transitional period of the WTO panel has already expired and the European Commission has not supported a appeal procedure. Therefore, in November 2007, we made further discussions with representatives of the social partners, the relevant Federal Ministries and NGOs. I think we have achieved significant successes in maintaining the freedom of choice for the Austrians. Due to the voluntary general abandonment of food trade in GMO products and the voluntary support of MON810 and T25 by the feed industry, the expectations of the consumer sector are ultimately fully fulfilled. Products from organic farming as well as those products which are equipped with the quality mark of the ARGE genetic engineering-free refer to the rules I have adopted on the non-genotechnic production method of the Codex Commission, which have now also been revised and are in force since the beginning of May. In the course of the discussions in autumn, we were also signalled by the Commission that import bans could continue to be maintained for the purpose of cultivation. By intervening between the regulation now to be adopted by me on an import ban for the purpose of cultivation and the precautionary laws adopted by the federal states, the cultivation of MON812 and T25 is still de facto impossible. I do not understand, however, that I would like to stress that there is still a great need for action in the field of environmental safety and risk research in Austria. it exists. A corresponding research capacity is virtually non-existent at the Austrian universities. I would point out that in recent years I have conducted a number of studies in the field of environmental safety research, toxicology and allergology, as well as in other areas in the context of my department's order and security research. An important part of these studies will be awarded and supervised in cooperation with the Federal Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry, Environment and Water Management. I have also commissioned the establishment of a research advisory board on risk research and benefit analysis in green genetic engineering. A corresponding legal basis for this is provided by the Federal Ministry Act, and the financial provision of a relevant research advisory board must be ensured in the context of the budgetary negotiations and through the cooperation of the relevant departments. This is a task that I also devote very seriously. The establishment of an interdisciplinary research body is becoming increasingly necessary, as the European legal situation will no longer allow national states to adopt unilateral bans. At present, 26 GMOs are authorised for use in food and feed. Another 42 constructions are waiting for approval. In future, we will have to introduce high-quality arguments into the authorisation procedure in order to obtain European bans. To do this, we need these research organisations and we need to increase them – I would like to have this again and again on this small subject, which is becoming an increasingly important issue for us in Austria and also in Europe – the discussion also with the approval authority, EFSA. The interdisciplinary research team should be able to draw up the relevant questions and to obtain, in the medium term, sound arguments that stand up to the Authority’s examination. In order to adopt a ban on MON863, I can say that I am prepared, in accordance with the precautionary principle, to have a temporary ban on imports due to the insufficient data for me on this construction. With regard to the necessary scientific reasons for notifying this Regulation to the European Commission, I have now also given a further study on the MON863 dossier, which I expect to see in these days. At the level of the European Union, both at the technical level in the relevant bodies and in cooperation with Italy at the Environmental Councils in June and December 2007, we pointed out the lack of adequate safety assessment by MON863 for us by EFSA. I would like to stress that in these individual questions several Member States are always supporting us. As regards the prohibition of the various rape lines, it should be noted that, like in the case of the rape line GT73, which had already been banned by my predecessor Maria Rauch-Kallatim in 2006, I also have a ban on the rape lines in question. Finally, four points: The abolition of import bans will not have an impact on consumers due to the voluntary abandonment of GMO products from the food and feed industry. Due to the organic production and the “gentech-free” seal, the freedom of choice for the Austrians remains. We need to pool our resources to ensure that the bans on cultivation, which are provided by the state's precautionary laws and the relevant regulations at federal level, are also sustainable. And we build an interdisciplinary risk research and benefit analysis to draw up high-quality results and findings in order to resist critical voices. I am also pleased that there is such a great consensus here. That is good for this country and that is good for Europe!

ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e20123	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! The draft law on the Shipping Act, which we have before us, transposes the EU Directive on Harmonised Inland Maritime Information Services into national law on the Community's inland waterways. The obligation to provide water information and the obligation to transmit electronic messages and automated identity and positioning messages from ships shall be provided. The draft legislation also contains rules on the basis of a need for renewal and adaptation of various legislation in the areas of maritime equipment, shipping and navigation. Currently, any extension of fleet capacity needs a new concession. The award of the ship concessions is tailored to the needs. In the future, professional qualifications are necessary for the exercise of the transport industry. What's a little bothering me in this context is: According to the current legal situation, in the event of similar serious infringements as in other transport regulations, such as the driver's right to drive, the withdrawal of the ship's right to be authorised is to be carried out on account of lack of roadworthiness. This withdrawal should be explicitly provided for. In doing so, withdrawal of the ID card for the duration of six months, in the first recurrence case for the duration of one year and in the second recurrence case is provided for indefinitely. The holder of a certificate of competence shall be withdrawn from the certificate at 0,5 per mille for a vehicle, a floater or an association of commercial shipping. However, if a person leads a vehicle, a floater or an association of non-commercial shipping with a certificate of qualification, this is only the case with 0.8 promille. There's a certain difference. For me, it is incomprehensible that these provisions and the penalties concerning the impairment of alcohol in shipping were not laid down in the light of the provisions of the driving licence law. There are no adequate measures to be taken in the event of alcohol impairment. In particular, account must also be taken of the increased risk potential of maritime freight transport and the associated protection requirements for the population and the environment. We remember the great environmental disaster caused by the “Exxon Valdez. ”

ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e20190	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! High house! The amendment to the Shipping Act, which is to be voted today, implements a whole series of measures. It takes into account citizens' interests and transforms EU law into national law. This is essentially the EU Directive on IWT, in short RIS. Information services shall be harmonised, on the one hand, in order to achieve the Authority's obligation to provide water information, on the other hand to produce regulatory authorisations for the introduction of electronic communications and automated identity and positioning notifications from ships. Of course, the transmitted data is defined free of charge, taking into account data protection. The implementation of this Directive contributes to a more efficient and safer use of the inland waterway network and a necessary measure in national transport policy. I am glad that this amendment is now taking place. Furthermore, this Act also regulates the improvement of the classification of training practices of boat leaders and the facilitation of access to the shipping industry. The latter in particular ensures that companies are not inhibited in their development. The increased use of economic and ecologically favourable inland navigation must also be able to be promoted in the future. It is precisely in times of climate debate that alternatives from the street to the environmentally friendly transport of goods must be designed and made more competitive. It is important to me, of course, that this matter of law should also extend the derogation for the German army's disaster assistance. This will continue to ensure the use of the federal army's watercraft in the event of serious elementary events. In recent years, we have been shown that our Austrian federal army is an essential part of the rescue mission in the event of disasters on water as well as on land. It is gratifying that this law will continue to allow and ensure. There is also an increased regulation on driving vehicles under alcohol influence. Alcohol at the tax level must not be a cavalier's offence in shipping and must be subject to appropriate penalties. Ladies and gentlemen, it is now generally forbidden in Austria to put a vehicle into service in a condition affected by alcohol. I see it as our duty to create legal framework conditions here, and I am glad that these are now created by this law. I am particularly pleased that this law also enables us to implement citizens' interests. Waterbikes represent a noise problem for anaerobics. These are a special burden in recreational areas on the Danube. This law also introduces this problem of a solution. All in all, this law is a round thing. And it is to congratulate Mrs State Secretary and her staff very much on this. Thank you.

One has to distinguish between the depth of the discharge and the depth of the water, which is 2.50 meters, which is then 2.80 meters. Of course, the Danube is 2 to 3 centimetres each year, and this natural depth has to be prevented. We apply very special material on the Danube sole, so this decline is prevented. At the same time, well-appointed banks in recent years, which have also caused criticism by environmental organisations, are being reversed. And most importantly: At certain places, care is taken to ensure that the necessary water can flow into the sensitive area of the Donauen National Park – of course under the premise that no groundwater risk may occur. ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e20638	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to stress once again that this law on safety uses the new communications technologies and implements the technical achievements of environmental safety. The aim of this law is to update and adapt existing legal standards, particularly in the field of administrative practice or in the field of communication technologies – it has already been mentioned several times – which implements a 2005 Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on Harmonised Inland Shipping Information Services. [[President Dr Glawischnig-Piesczek shall take over the chairmanship.]] Among other things, it concerns the provision of water information and messages by the authorities, but also the electronic transmission of messages and automated identity and positioning messages from ships. It is essential that the ship's data are compiled on the basis of data protection and that the conditions for it are defined under which these data are stored and passed on. I would like to address another point, namely the establishment of handling facilities for dangerous liquid goods. These are, for example, those who do not mix with water and ignite at more than 55 degrees Celsius. These goods require increased caution in transport, as accidents cause devastating consequences, especially when transport ships lose such liquids due to an accident. For this reason, the handling facilities for such dangerous goods have so far been banned outside of port basins. From today's point of view, this strict ban is no longer viable in view of technical progress, and so the new law provides for the establishment of ports of handling for dangerous liquid goods outside port basins. It has already been mentioned that access to the transport sector has been facilitated by the new legislation to be adopted today. So far, all the data needed to be made available to the public. continuing fleet capacity a new concession, which has certainly weakened the economic location and also presented a barrier for the company. If the award of concessions has originally been geared to the needs, the professional suitability for the exercise of the transport industry is now the priority. Improvements in training practice have also been mentioned with regard to the ship's guide or the ship's leader. So far, one who is trying to run 20 metres long vehicles has been able to complete the training practice on a ship at least ten meters long. However, this is already covered by another patent category and means that the intended vehicle would be twice as long. The safety factor has been increased here by more than ten metres of training vehicles in the case in question. Finally, I would like to say that I believe that a revision of the rules will continue to be necessary in order to adapt inland waterway transport to economic and technical conditions and to further reduce bureaucracy. I am thinking of facilitating access to ship leader patents or the recognition of foreign patents. I believe that in times of climate protection, shipping, inland waterway transport should be made more attractive and simply much better used. Thank you.

[[19:16:03]] 11th@item: inlistbox Point Report of the Transport Committee on the Government Template (451 e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law amending the Federal Law on Transport Labour Inspection (VAIG) (545 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e20958	We're on our way to the 11th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. As the first speaker, I shall give the floor to Mr Heinzl for 4 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e20985	Madam President. Mrs. State Secretary! High house! The Transport Labour Inspectorate is responsible for workers of the railways, trams, cable cars, the Austrian Post Office, the Postbuses, as well as airports and airlines. As part of its activity, the labour inspectorate in 2007 inspected 620 establishments, reviewing over 44 400 people. The traffic labour inspector is working successfully. That is indeed to be proved. The number of accidents at work has fallen by 37 percent over the last ten years. Today's new law aims at simplifying the work of the labour inspectorate and reducing administrative burden, first of all leaving the reporting obligation to the supervisory authority for minor measures. The reporting obligation so far referred to 1995. Since then, the legal situation has fundamentally changed through the new Railway Act. The withdrawal of the reporting obligation will result in administrative simplification while maintaining – and this is particularly important – the existing level of safety. And second: The Commission's fee margin has been increased from €50 to €100, and this adjustment of the bagatll limit ensures that expensive pick-up operations for minor contributions are avoided. High house! At present, as you know, the European Union's Community initiative for greater safety and health at work is under way. Due to compliance with worker protection, the number of accidents at work is to be reduced by 25 percent by 2013. However, this objective can only be achieved with the support of the labour inspectorate. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! The SPÖ is of course very pleased to agree with the changes contained in this novel.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e21027	Next speaker: Mr Höfinger. 2 minutes speaking time. - Please.

She was immediately put in a prison, 24 hours without the possibility of contacting lawyers, relatives or acquaintances. She was interrogated, photographed, fingerprinted, she had to get out without sleep and without food because she had crossed a stay in the USA for three weeks, and that in 1995. I think you should think about it again. I hope so. ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e21456	Next, Mr Haberzettl. 4 minutes of voluntary time limit. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-05-08-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e21477	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear Secretary of State! This agreement between the European Community and its Member States, of the one part, and the United States of America, of the other part, is actually a mixed agreement. That is to say, it falls within the competence of the Community and, on the other hand, in those of the Member States. This agreement is based on the intention to create a more open airspace in which air carriers from both sides can settle freely and compete with each other under harmonised, equivalent legal conditions. This agreement represents the most extensive agreement in air transport that has ever been negotiated. The two markets are the largest air transport markets in the world. The 60 percent of air transport accounts for these airspace areas. This agreement offers the opportunity to get closer together, and especially for European air carriers, the opportunity to conquer new markets. This agreement is only a first step. Further negotiations on further arrangements between the Community, Member States and the United States will be opened in Ljubljana shortly. What are the basic results so far? A wide-ranging opening of air transport markets between the European Community and the USA, with the most open pricing possible, extensive harmonisation in the application of the legal systems and the production of the Community's conformity with existing air transport agreements. In concrete terms, this Agreement contains provisions in the areas of market access, ownership and control, commercial opportunities, tariffs and charges, user charges, pricing, aviation and air safety, State aid, environmental protection, consumer protection and computerised booking systems and institutional arrangements. The concrete effects on European air carriers include, inter alia, that any European airline will be able to operate direct flights to the USA from any location in Europe in the future and that any restrictions relating to the route tariffs or the number of flights will be lifted. For European airlines, there is now the possibility to operate flights across the US also in third countries – the so-called fifth freedom – and there is now also the possibility to operate freight flights between the USA and any third country, with flights not to start or end in Europe. I think that these are very important and important steps in a humanised air space between Europe and America, and I believe that we should continue along this path quite quickly and successfully.
